先週（11/2〜11/9）発表の主な経済指標
⽶国雇⽤は安定も、AI曝露度の⾼い職業で若年雇⽤が弱含
み。雇⽤不安が⺠主党躍進を後押しした可能性。AI関連株が
調整するなか、韓国中銀は投資減速のアジアへの波及を警戒
所定内給与は物価上昇率を上回る伸びが持続。冬季ボーナス
も押し上げ。電気・ガス料⾦補助再開は消費の追い⾵に。⼀⽅、
消費回復が進めば基調的な物価上昇圧⼒を強める可能性
注目点② 賃上げの波及と物価対策が日本の消費を下支え注目点① 米AI投資拡大の裏で広がる「不安」と「リスク」
⾮農業部⾨雇⽤者数は市場予想を上回り、失業
率も低下。ISM景況指数は製造業・⾮製造業とも
に拡⼤圏を維持。雇⽤・企業活動の底堅さの⼀⽅
で、消費者マインドは持ち直すも依然として低⽔準

（出所）米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
失業率
米国：雇用統計は良好な結果。一方で、賃金上昇もインフレ見合いで見劣り
 10月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差＋16.5万人と市場予想（同＋6.5万人）を大きく上回る結果
―過去2カ月も合わせて8.4万人の上方修正。業種別では専門ビジネスサービスやヘルスケアが全体をけん引
― 時間当たり賃金も前月比＋0.4％（前年比＋3.1％）と着実に増加。ただしインフレ率に割り負けている状況が継続
 失業率は4.1％と前月（4.2％）から小幅低下も、失業期間は長期化
― 失業期間の中央値は11.9週（9月：同11.5週）と長期化。27週以上の長期失業者も増加基調維持。企業の採用率は低下

傾向が続いており、労働者が失業状態から脱しにくい傾向が継続
3
（出所）米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

（年）
米国：AI普及の影響は若年層に偏在。雇用機会への不安が拡大
 AI曝露度別の雇用指数をみると、22～34歳では曝露度の高い職業の雇用が相対的に弱含み
― AIの影響がキャリア形成段階の若年層に偏っている可能性
 若年層では、AIによる雇用代替に加え、物価高など生活面の不安も根強い状況
― AIを否定的に捉える割合も若い世代ほど高く、AI普及への警戒感がうかがえる状況
4
（注） 上図の若年層とは18～29歳。経済状況は回答者の主観的な評価

（出所） Harvard Youth Poll、ABC News、Washington Post、Ipsosより、みずほ総合研
究所調査部作成
AI曝露度別雇用指数
年齢別AIの認識：「AIは社会に悪い」と回答した割合
（注） 1．AI曝露度とは各職業の業務が生成AIの影響を受ける度合いを示す。職業を曝露度
に応じて5分位に分類し、低・中・高はそれぞれ第1・第3・第5分位を指す
2．年齢層別の雇用指数は、米国雇用統計の家計調査における年齢別雇用者数をウェ
イトに加重平均して算出。年齢区分が一致しない部分は、各階級内の雇用者数が均
等に分布すると仮定して按分
（出所）スタンフォード大学デジタル経済研究所、ADP Researchより、みずほ総合研究所調査
部作成

若年層（22～34歳）
若年層が感じる経済的脅威
若年層ほどAIへの反感が強い
若年層に偏る雇用への影響

（年/月）
米国：若年層の経済的不安が中間選挙の投票行動にも影響か
 2026年中間選挙では「物価高」や「雇用」への不安が若年層の主要な関心事項に
― こうした経済的不安が共和党政権への不満につながった可能性
― 年齢別の投票動向では、若年層を中心に民主党支持が優勢。生活費負担や将来の雇用機会への不安が投票行動に
影響した模様
― なお、議会でも世代交代が進展。若い世代の政策選好が今後の政策形成に与える影響にも注目

### Page 7

（参考）民主党が掲げる主な政策
 勝利した民主党は、若年層の不満とも重なる生活費負担の軽減（アフォーダビリティ）を共通の政策課題に位置付け
 具体的な政策手段を巡っては、中道派と左派・進歩派の間で違い
― 中道派は競争促進や規制緩和、財政規律を重視する一方、左派・進歩派は大企業への規制・課税強化や政府支出の
拡大を主張。今後の党内調整が焦点に
6

（年）

アジア：AI投資減速の影響が域内の貿易ネットワークを通じて波及するリスク
 韓国中銀はAI関連投資の拡大に伴う過剰投資やレバレッジの高まり、循環金融などの金融リスクを警戒
― AI産業の期待収益の過大評価や最終的なリスク所在把握が困難になり、リスクが過小評価される可能性があると指摘
― また、米国企業のAI投資が減速する悲観シナリオでは、韓国の半導体輸出が大幅に減少し、GDP成長率を0.4％Pt押し
下げると試算
― 韓国のAI関連財貿易は中国・台湾との取引が4割超。アジア域内で形成された貿易ネットワークを通じて、AI投資減速の
影響が広範に波及する可能性

日本：物価対策の再開が家計負担を軽減。消費の回復を後押し
9
 政府は冬場の電気・ガス料金補助の再開を決定。標準的な家庭で計7,000円程度の負担軽減を見込む
― 一方、ガソリン価格の補助は段階的に縮小（基準額を毎月5円ずつ引き上げ）。電気・ガス料金補助金の効果を一部減殺
 補助金を巡る政策変更は、コアCPIの前年比を最大0.4％程度押し下げ
― 賃金上昇に加え、政策による物価負担軽減が家計の実質購買力を下支え
― 物価対策がCPIを押し下げる一方、消費回復を通じて基調的な物価上昇圧力が強まる可能性には留意が必要

（注） ガソリンと同額の補助を実施している軽油・重油・灯油、ガソリン補助額の4割相当
の支援を実施している航空機燃料については未定

欧州：10月消費者物価はエネルギー主導で加速。企業は販売価格の引き上げを計画
10
 10月消費者物価（HICP）は前年比＋4.0％と、エネルギー主導で前月（同＋3.8％）から加速
― 一方、コアは同＋2.5％と前月から不変。エネルギー高の二次的な影響はいまだ顕在化せず
 コスト上昇圧力が高まるなか、企業は販売価格の引き上げを計画。高止まりするインフレにECBは警戒姿勢を維持
― 企業の販売価格見通しは7～9月期に上昇。カジミール・スロバキア中銀総裁は、過去数カ月にわたるコスト上昇圧力の

中国：政策金利をリバースレポ翌日物に変更。銀行貸出金利の新たな決定メカニズムを模索
 中国人民銀行は政策金利をリバースレポ翌日物に変更（11/2）。短期市場金利の誘導精度を高める狙い
― リバースレポ7日物から切り替え、翌日物のオペを毎営業日実施。最優遇貸出金利（LPR）も翌日物金利を参照へ
― インターバンク市場における債券担保付レポ翌日物金利（DR001）が誘導目標と明言。短期金利の安定化を図る考え
 銀行貸出金利の新たな決定メカニズムを模索。DR001を参照して貸出金利を決定する方法を試験的に導入

― 背景には、銀行貸出の指標金利であるLPRが「最優遇」から乖離し、LPRより低利での貸出比率が上昇している実態
― 今後、財務力のある企業は銀行と協議の上でDR001かLPRを選択し、小規模企業や個人には引き続きLPRを適用へ

中国：景況感は再び悪化し、2カ月ぶりの節目割れ。製造業は需給ともに振るわず
 10月の景況感は再び悪化。総合PMIは49.7と前月（50.7）から大幅に低下し、2カ月ぶりの節目割れ
― うち非製造業PMIが49.7と前月（50.2）から低下も、宿泊やスポーツ・娯楽が持ち直し、情報サービスは堅調だった
― 建設業PMIは48.0と前月（50.3）から急低下。中国物流購買聯合会は建設工事の着工が遅れ気味と指摘
 製造業PMIは49.3と前月（50.1）から低下。供給側の生産、需要側の受注がともに振るわず

― 生産が49.8（前月：51.7）、新規受注が49.4（同：50.5）、新規輸出受注も48.7（同：50.0）といずれも節目の50を下回る
― 中東情勢に伴う価格上昇に一服感。原材料価格は56.8（同：60.8）、出荷価格は51.7（同：54.0）まで戻す

中国：PPIは生産財が急上昇も、川下への価格転嫁進まず。CPIは食品価格が下押し要因に
 10月のPPIは前年比＋5.7％（前月：同＋5.0％）。中東情勢の影響による資源・エネルギー関連業種の価格上昇続く
― 石油・石炭加工（同＋29.9％）と非鉄金属加工（同＋18.6％）、化学品（同＋16.4％）が引き続き高い伸び

― 他方、燃料価格高騰により、ガソリンは同＋22.4％（前月：同＋16.6％）まで上昇し、全体を＋0.63％Pt押し上げ
― 食品・エネルギーを除くコアは同＋0.8％（前月：＋1.0％）と弱いが、通信機器はメモリー高騰で同＋16.1％の伸び

### Page 15

アジア：ASEAN5の製造業景況感は概ね堅調。原油高など仕入コスト上昇の影響は残存
 ASEAN5の10月の製造業景況感は、フィリピンを除き改善圏。受注・生産は好調も、コスト高が継続
― タイ、ベトナムは高水準で推移し、インドネシアは回復が鮮明。一方、フィリピンが基準となる50を下回る水準
― S&Pはリリース資料において、新規受注の増加が生産を支えたものの、仕入コスト上昇と原料調達・物流の制約を指摘
 ASEAN5では、生産者物価（PPI）が高止まり。原油高の影響が残存
― 原油高が続く中、石炭・石油に加えて化学やプラ・ゴム等の製品価格にも上昇圧力が継続
― 仕入コストの高止まりが、企業収益や生産活動の重荷となるリスクに留意が必要

 ASEAN5の一部の国で財政収支が悪化

― フィリピンは税収の伸び悩み、タイは景気下支えに向けた公共投資拡大や予算執行の加速などにより歳出が大きく増加
 各国とも財政面の制約を抱え、追加的な景気下支え余地は限定的
― タイは公的債務残高が法定上限（GDP比70％）に接近。マレーシアも政府債務残高が高水準
― フィリピンは財政赤字の縮小が課題。インドネシアは財政赤字上限（GDP比3％）が制約要因

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國聯準會如市場預期，維持政策利率不變。決策官員仍對通膨保持擔憂，也承
認美國經濟正以「強勁速度」成長。聯準會主席 Kevin Warsh 在會後談話中，並
未明確排除進一步緊縮政策的可能，激勵市場對 12 月升息的預期升溫。數據方
面，美國第三季 GDP 年化成長率達 3.4%，高於前值的 2.2%，亦優於市場預期
的 3.2%，印證美國經濟動能強勁。此外，9 月 PCE 與核心 PCE 符合預期，但凸
顯通膨壓力持續偏高。在「通膨高企」與「經濟強韌」雙重因素驅動下，市場預
期聯準會將於 12 月升息，為美元指數提供有力支撐。惟近期受 AI 產業股票波
動加劇影響，部分資金轉入美債避險，導致美元指數在高檔面臨調節性賣壓。
⚫ 儘管受能源價格走揚影響，9 月歐元區通膨率升至 3.8%，但在薪資壓力維持溫
和，且歐洲央行尚未觀察到第二輪通膨效應全面擴散的情況下，歐洲央行上週決
議維持政策利率不變。通膨回升令市場預期歐洲央行將在下次會議升息，為歐元

提供部分支撐，但考量歐元區經濟成長表現仍落後美國，在美元基本面強勁的抑
制下，歐元反彈力道相對有限。目前歐元最大的潛在風險在於部分國家的債券市
場壓力尚未消散，市場對歐洲財政風險仍保持高度警戒。
⚫ 日本央行維持政策利率於 1.25%不變，並且首次明確表示已達成其 2%的通膨目
標；過去日本央行雖認為通膨改善，但始終強調尚未穩定達標，如今首次正式承
認達成 2%目標，代表未來升息門檻進一步下降。此外，日本央行將經濟成長預
測維持在 0.7%至 0.8%，並小幅上修 CPI 預測，令市場預期日央 12 月有望再次
升息，日本貨幣政策正常化將有助日圓結束疲軟走勢。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Nov/2/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.390，低點在 32.676，以 32.478 作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 3 分。 
上週台幣先升後貶，周初，市場等待聯準會利率決議，交投偏謹慎觀望，而外
資在前周大幅匯出之後，轉為雙向進出，台幣止跌回穩，最高來到 32.390 的周內高
點；然而周間，美國科技股財報表現失色，台股賣壓沉重，外資賣超台股並再度轉
為大幅匯出，台幣走貶，最低來到 32.676，不過匯價逢高吸引出口商賣壓出籠，加
上央行提供流動性，台幣貶幅收斂，終場在央行調節之下收在 32.478。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣偏弱震盪。就國際市場來看，聯準會 10 月利率決策維持利率不
變，但有兩名官員主張升息 1 碼，且聯準會表示通膨仍處於高檔，顯示聯準會對通
膨仍保持警戒，市場預期美國利率將維持高檔，台美利差仍維持在相對高位，不利
台幣表現；另一方面，美國科技股財報表現不如預期，引發市場對 AI 投資回報的疑
慮，對台股也帶來壓力。就台灣市場來看，外資持續調節台股並大幅匯出，10 月累
計賣超將近兆元，資金匯出壓力大，不過一方面央行持續進場調節，加上匯價逢高
預計吸引出口商進場拋匯，料將限制台幣貶值速度。綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢
偏弱。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.300-32.800

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Nov/2/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣持續走升。周初市場等待美國聯準會利率決議，交投偏向觀望，境
外人民幣主要於 6.6600 上方震盪；周間聯準會如預期維持利率不變，美元指數自 102
上方回落，加上中國出口結匯需求持續提供支撐，境外人民幣突破 6.6600 整數關口；
週五人行中間價續創近三年九個月新高，最高來到 6.7138，顯示中國人行允許人民
幣溫和升值的走勢，境外人民幣一度升破 6.6500 整數關口，最高來到 6.6487，終場
收在 6.6528，週線升值 162 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6558，週線升值 146 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣偏升。就中國國內市場來看，10 月官方製造業 PMI 降至 49.3，
重新落入景氣收縮區間，反映中國內需及製造業動能仍有待改善，不過 9 月出口年
增 36.3%，出口商結匯需求持續提供人民幣支撐，加上人行中間價續創近三年九個
月新高，預計將持續帶動人民幣走升；就國際市場來看，聯準會 10 月利率決策維持
利率不變，但有兩名官員主張升息 1 碼，顯示聯準會對通膨仍保持警戒，市場預期
美國利率將維持高檔，美中利差維持在相對高位，將限制人民幣升值的速度與幅度；
最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期均線向下發散，匯價維持在五日均
線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，顯示空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣
偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6200-6.6700

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Nov/02/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 上漲。週初，美伊衝突仍陷僵局支撐美元避險買盤，USD/JPY 最
高來到 158.34；週間，聯準會公布利率維持利率不變，USD/JPY 持續在 158 附近盤
整；臨近週末，日本央行公布利率維持不變並釋放偏鷹訊號，USD/JPY 最低來到
156.94，終場收在 157.53，週線上漲 72 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本央行於上周五宣布利
率維持在 1.25%不變，並表示隨著基礎通膨率大致達到 2%，央行必須持續控制通
膨穩定於此目標，且再次強調貨幣政策已進入更為緊縮階段，未來每隔數月即可能
升息。此外，日本央行在季度報告中，亦將截至 2028 年 3 月財年的核心消費者通
膨預估自三個月前預測的 2.4%上調至 2.6%。日本央行偏鷹立場令市場預期日本最
快將於 12 月再次升息，預計將有利日圓表現。二、聯準會宣布維持利率於 3.75%-
4%區間不變，惟因通膨仍處高位，聯準會重申將致力實現 2%的長期通膨區間， 
FedWatch 顯示 12 月升息機率約七成，美元持穩，預計將限縮 USD/JPY 下行空
間。三、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY 各天期均線趨平，近期匯價大致
在 156 至 158 間震盪，匯價暫無明確方向性。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現
區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日圓：156.00-158.50 
前週交易區間 
156.63 158.34

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Nov/02/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，美伊衝突持續以及法國政經情勢動盪等情況下，
歐元走跌，最低來到 1.1111；週間，聯準會和歐洲央行相繼公布利率決議均維持不
變，歐洲央行發表言論偏鷹派，歐元止跌回升至 1.1214；臨近週末，歐元區公布經
濟數據好壞參半，歐元自高點回落，終場收在 1.1158，週線下跌 41 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行如市場預期宣布
存款利率維持在 2.5%不變，並警告能源成本持續上漲將對通膨造成上行風險，暗示
可能在 12 月再次升息，升息預期升溫預計將支撐歐元走勢。二、歐元區第三季 GDP
年增 1.3%，高於市場預期的 1.1%，顯示在能源價格持續上漲的不利因素下，經濟仍
展現韌性，預計有利歐元表現。三、聯準會宣布維持利率在 3.75%-4%不變，並重申
致力控制通膨的立場。此外，美國公布第三季 GDP 成長 3.4%，優於市場預期的 3.2%，
9 月核心 PCE 年增率自前月的 3%上升至 3.1%，在美國經濟強勁及通膨仍高的情況
下，市場預期聯準會將於 12 月升息一碼，美元持穩，預計將限縮歐元走升空間。四、
技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 各天期均線向下發散，不過近期匯價在多次向
下測試 1.1120 附近均未有效跌破，顯示下方仍有買盤支撐。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD
本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1050-1.1270 
前週交易區間 
1.1103 1.1226

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.10.27-115.10.30 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.66% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109R 2.03% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.845%~1.846%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，時序邁入月底，銀行間已大致調整好積數，且央行未明顯
增發定存單，令市場資金充裕。近兩日股市回落，令週五資金需求上揚，惟供
需尚屬平衡，因此短率持穩於近期區間。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.825% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.66%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 本週台債市場交投依舊清淡，10 年期指標券 A15109R 等殖系統成交在 2.03%，
5 年期指標券 A15108 議價成交在 1.845%~1.846%。 
美債利率自波段高點回落，且央行總裁楊金龍本週立法院發言維持近來對通
膨容忍度變寬之態度，惟市場對央行升息預期並未完全消除，買盤追價動力有
限，預期台債殖利率維持區間整理，關注下週 30 年期公債標售果，預估 5 年
券區間為 1.80%~1.85%，10 年券區間為 1.99%~2.04%。

廣達(2382 TT) Trading Buy

AI 伺服器展望維持不變，投資建議 Trading Buy 
投資建議：廣達目前未見主要 CSP 客戶調整 AI 伺服器訂單，
且考慮到 CSP 對於記憶體、CPU 和 PCB 等關鍵零組件的鎖料
能力，我們預期 Vera Rubin 將於如原先預期於 1Q27 放量，2027
年營運展望維持正向，然而考慮到近期市場對於資料中心建置
的疑慮升溫而可能抑制評價，因此我們給予廣達 Trading Buy 的
投資建議，目標價 324 元。 
3Q26 營收 1.12 兆元，稅後 EPS 6.92 元：3Q26 營收 1.12 兆元，
QoQ+7.6%；毛利率 4.75%， QoQ-0.27ppts；營業利益 322.04 億
元，QoQ-6.0%；歸屬母公司淨利 284.22 億元，QoQ-0.8%；稅後
EPS 6.92 元。 
PC 市場需求疲弱，伺服器業務支撐營收季增： 3Q26 筆電出貨
960 萬台，QoQ-17%，考慮到筆電零組件漲價帶動 ASP 提高，
我們預估 PC 營收季減高個位數。通用型伺服器營收優於預期，
除 2H26 AI Agent 強勁的需求外，ASP 亦由於零組件漲價而提
升。AI 伺服器部分出貨量較 2Q26 微幅下滑，但同樣受零組件
漲價帶動 ASP 提升影響，預估 AI 伺服器營收仍季增低個位數。 
2027 年伺服器展望不變：近期市場對於限制資料中心相關法案
的擔憂升溫，然而廣達表示目前客戶方面並未調整訂單，就現
階段來看主要 CSP 的資本支出尚未放緩，我們預估 2027 年 AI
伺服器展望將維持不變。我們預期 1Q27 Vera Rubin 將可如先
前預期放量，帶動 2027 年 AI 伺服器營收高雙位數增長。考慮
到 AI Agent 的需求持續成長，預期 2027 年通用型伺服器全年
營收呈雙位數年增。

2 群益投顧 
  ESG 
廣達積極推動環境、社會及公司治理（ESG）永續發展，並將節能減碳、人
才培育及供應鏈管理納入企業營運策略。環境方面，公司於 2024 年通過科
學基礎減碳目標倡議（SBTi）審查，承諾以 2022 年為基準，2030 年前將範
疇一及範疇二溫室氣體排放量降低 42%、範疇三降低 25%，並以 2050 年達
成淨零排放為長期目標。公司亦持續推動再生能源使用及能源管理，2024 年
綠電使用占總用電量 47.51%，並設定 2030 年再生能源使用比例達 50%的目
標。 
社會責任方面，廣達重視員工權益、職業安全及人才培育，遵循責任商業聯
盟（ RBA）行為準則，並透過廣達文教基金會及廣藝基金會推動教育與藝文
活動。公司治理方面，廣達持續強化風險管理、資訊安全及供應鏈永續管理，
並參照 TCFD 及 SASB 等國際框架揭露永續資訊，以提升營運透明度及長期
經營韌性。 
公司簡介 
廣達電腦（Quanta Computer Inc.， 2382 TT）成立於 1988 年，總部位於台灣
桃園，為全球主要電子產品原廠設計製造（ODM）廠商之一，主要業務涵蓋
筆記型電腦、伺服器、雲端運算設備及其他智慧電子產品。公司早期以筆記
型電腦代工為主要業務，憑藉產品研發、供應鏈管理及全球製造能力，建立
與國際品牌客戶的長期合作關係，近年則積極擴展雲端運算及人工智慧相
關業務。 
廣達透過旗下雲達科技（QCT）布局資料中心市場，提供伺服器、儲存設備、
網路交換器及整機櫃解決方案，並隨大型雲端服務供應商（CSP）擴 大 AI 基
礎建設投資，逐步深化 AI 伺服器業務布局。相較傳統筆電代工，AI 伺服器
對系統整合、散熱、電源管理及高速互連技術要求較高，帶動產品複雜度及
單機價值提升，成為公司營收成長的重要驅動因素。 
展望未來，廣達持續推進 AI 伺服器及機櫃級系統整合能力，並透過全球生
產據點布局，強化供應鏈韌性及在地化交付能力。隨 AI 運算需求持續擴張，
伺服器業務可望維持公司中長期成長動能，惟仍須關注 CSP 資本支出、產
品轉換時程及新平台量產效率對營運的影響。 
3Q26 營收 1.12 兆元，稅後 EPS 7.08 元：3Q26 營收 1.12 兆元，QoQ+7.6%，
YoY+125.2%；毛利率 4.75%，QoQ-0.27ppts，YoY-2.10ppts；營業利益 322.04
億元，QoQ-6.0%，YoY+75.3%，營益率 2.89%，QoQ-0.41ppts，YoY-0.82ppts；
歸屬母公司淨利 284.22 億元，QoQ-0.8%， YoY+73.0%；稅後 EPS 7.08 元。
PC 市場需求疲弱，伺服器業務支撐營收季增： 3Q26 筆電出貨 960 萬台，
QoQ-17%， YoY-24%，出貨量略優於 2Q26 預期的 925 萬台，考慮到筆電零
組件漲價帶動 ASP 提高，我們預估 PC 營收季減高個位數。通用型伺服器營
收優於預期，主要原因除 2H26 AI Agent 強勁的需求外，亦由於 ASP 受零
組件漲價影響而提升。AI 伺服器部分出貨量較 2Q26 微幅下滑，但同樣受零
組件漲價帶動 ASP 提升影響，預估 AI 伺服器營收仍季增低個位數。 
零組件漲價及產品組合改變導致毛利率承壓：由於(1)筆電持續因成本上揚
而壓縮獲利，且出貨數下滑不利經濟規模(2)雖 AI 伺服器出貨量季減，但由

### Page 3

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 12 日

3 群益投顧 
ASP 提升驅動營收增長，造成毛利率稀釋(3)通用型伺服器出貨量小幅季增，
且 ASP 提升。綜上原因導致 3Q26 毛利率季減 0.27ppts，帶動營益率季減
0.41ppts，此一情景預估將延續至 2027 年，且我們預估 Vera Rubin 世代平
台 ASP 較 GB300 系列接近倍增，雖營收與獲利將可持續上升，但未來 margin
將持續承壓。雖目前有數個專案採用 Consignment model，然而主要以通用
型伺服器與 ASIC 平台為主，且出貨形式由 L6 至 L11 皆有，營收貢獻相對
於採用 B/S 的整櫃 GPU 伺服器而言佔比極低。據我們了解目前已有確定一
個 Vera Rubin 的大型專案及數個規模較小的專案將採用 Consignment 
model，但預估占整體伺服器營收比重仍低，對於毛利率的改善效果有限。
由於 B/S 將稀釋毛利率，Consignment 則會使營收下滑，因此未來針對 ODM
的獲利能力的評估重點應轉向以營業利益年複合成長率(CAGR)或是 ROIC
為主。 
終止華亞科技園區收購，2028 年產能擴充計畫維持不變：廣達表示，由於
對廠方規格及交付時程等事項無法取得共識，因此撤銷友達華亞科技園區
廠房的收購案，原先規劃的產能將透過海外投資計畫或其他彈性應變方案，
維持 2028 年投產的計畫不變。廣達 3Q26 資本支出 128 億元，2026 前三季
累計資本支出達 346 億元，預估 2027 年資本支出介於 400-450 億元，投資
金額成長幅度收斂，將主要用於改良租賃廠房及電力升級。管理層表示 AI
擴廠計畫為配合 CSP 客戶未來 12-24 個月的需求規劃，且伺服器屬於客製
化接單生產(Build-to-Order)，非承擔庫存風險的標準品倍或生產，資本支出
主要投入的項目為廠房電力設施及高階冷卻系統等通用的基礎設施，因此
縱然單一客戶或是特定世代的 AI 伺服器平台需求放緩，亦可無縫轉換產能，
較不會出現過去供過於求時，閒置產能導致的折舊與維護費用等沉沒成本，
營運風險相對較小。 
2027 年伺服器展望不變：美國期中選舉後，市場對於限制資料中心相關法
案的擔憂升溫，然而廣達表示目前客戶方面並未調整訂單。廣達的客戶以五
大 CSP 為主，Neocloud 的占比不高，就現階段來看主要 CSP 的資本支出尚
未放緩，我們預估 2027 年 AI 伺服器展望將維持不變。3Q26 AI 伺服器出貨
量的下滑主要原因為新舊平台交替的陣痛期，預估 4Q26 伺服器出貨量仍將
下滑，但因 Vera Rubin 相較 GB300 近乎倍增的 ASP，AI 伺服器營收將維持
季增。此外，目前在記憶體、PCB 及 CPU 缺貨問題對於消費性產品及通用
型伺服器影響較大，但由於廣達至 2027 年的訂單人以五大 CSP 為主，且
CSP 巨頭鎖料能力較強，因此 AI 伺服器的長短料問題相較之下較輕微，我
們預期 1Q27 Vera Rubin將可如先前預期放量，帶動 2027 年 AI 伺服器營收
高雙位數增長。通用型伺服器部分，受惠 AI Agent 的應用落地帶動通用型
伺服器需求增長，預估 4Q26 通用型伺服器將持續季增。考慮到 AI Agent 的
需求持續成長，預期 2027 年通用型伺服器除 Share gain 外，亦受惠整體市
場需求提升帶來的成長，全年營收呈雙位數年增。 
管理層預期 2027 年筆電市場持續低迷：管理層預期 2027 年筆電需求將持
續受料件漲價及缺料等問題壓抑，導致 2027 年筆電出貨量下滑高個位數至
低雙位數左右，考慮到目前筆電營收占比已不足 15%，因此我們認為 2027
年合併營收將受伺服器的強勁成長帶動而年增。 
預估 4Q26 營收 1.19 兆元，稅後 EPS 7.19 元：預期 4Q26 筆電市場仍舊低
迷，AI 伺服器處於世代交替的轉換期，出貨量將持續下滑，但受 ASP 提升
影響而帶動 AI 伺服器營收季增，此外通用型伺服器出貨亦維持強勁。綜上

4 群益投顧 
所述，群益預估 4Q26 營收 1.19 兆元，QoQ+7.0%：毛利率 4.62%， QoQ-
0.13ppts，毛利續持續下滑主要受到 AI 伺服器 ASP 提升帶來的稀釋，以及
筆電出貨量下滑造成的規模經濟效果衰減所影響；營業利益 331.68 億元，
QoQ+3.0%，營益率 2.78%；歸屬母公司淨利 288.35 億元，QoQ+1.45%；稅
後 EPS 7.02 元。 
預估 2027 年營收 5.99 兆元，稅後 EPS 30.85 元：群益預估 2027 年合併營
收 5.99 兆元，YoY+44.1%；毛利率 4.18%，YoY-0.6ppts；營業利益 1,490.08
億元，YoY+21.5%，營益率 2.49%， YoY-0.5ppts；歸屬母公司淨利 1,266.99
億元，YoY+18.3%，稅後 EPS 30.85 元。 
投資建議：廣達目前未見主要 CSP 客戶調整 AI 伺服器訂單，且考慮到 CSP
對於記憶體、CPU 和 PCB 等關鍵零組件的鎖料能力，我們預期 Vera Rubin
將於如原先預期於 1Q27 放量，帶動 2027 年 AI 伺服器營收維持高雙位數成
長。通用型伺服器則受惠於 AI Agent 帶動運算需求及市占提升，預估全年
營收呈雙位數年增。預期伺服器強勁的成長將可抵銷筆電市場的低迷，帶動
2027 年營收年增，惟 Vera Rubin 的高單價將使毛利率及營益率承壓，但營
業利益亦將仍持續增長，整體營運展望維持正向，然而考慮到近期市場對於
資料中心建置的疑慮升溫而可能抑制評價，因此我們給予廣達 Trading Buy
的投資建議，目標價 324。

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 12 日

8 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 1

M
Update
Phison Electronics Corp  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 gross margin miss; our
conservative stance on Nand
modules playing out
Looking back to our February downgrade note: In AI boosts NAND demand, but
Asian module maker margins may soon compress, we noted that Phison's gross
margin could decline in 2Q26 or 3Q26, once it ran out of low-cost inventory.  While
Phison's low-cost inventory cushioned the consumer Nand module margin downside,
its overall NAND module margin started to miss in 3Q26, which means the AI

storage part of its business (eSSD, and cSSD for AI PC, together 40% of revenue)
may see margin decline.
Please see Exhibit 1  for our earnings review. The company's 3Q26 gross margin
declined to 59%, below our 63.5% and the street's 60.3%, also missing the
company's previous commentary about a sustainable gross margin in 3Q26 given its
low cost inventory. The company hopes its AiDaptive module and service business
can pick up and offset the margin pressure in the conventional module business in

the coming quarters.
Exhibit 1: 2Q Earnings Review

Phison Electronics Corp (8299.TWO)

12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.

universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

12-18 months.

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
좋은 실적이 상수인 구간 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 31.9% 증가한 104조 
6,150억원이다. 이전 전망 대비 증가한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 
이전 전망 대비 물량은 유사했지만 가격 상승폭은 예상보다 높았다. 26년 3분기 
영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 37.6% 증가한 83.3조원이다. 이전 전망을 상회한 
수준이다. 
실적이 끌고, 수급이 밀고 
지금까지는 높은 실적에 대한 불안보다는 먼 미래에 대한 불확실성을 앞서 주가에 
반영시키는 힘이 더 강하게 작용하다가, 주가가 1,190,000원을 찍은 이후 상승세를 
보이고 있다. 실적을 발표한 10월 28일도 10% 넘게 상승했다. 기대 이상의 실적과 
미국 반도체 시장 분위기가 좋았던 영향이다. 앞으로는 악재에 대해서 민감도가 떨
어지는 구간에 진입할 것으로 전망한다. 지나치게 하락한 주가와 밸류에이션이 버팀
목이 될 것이고, Anthropic의 IPO까지 발표되는 Big Tech 기업들의 실적은 시장의 
우려를 충분히 상쇄할 수 있을 것으로 기대한다. 추가로 진행되는 자사주 매수도 불
안한 수급을 해소할 수 있을 전망이다. 
목표주가는 괴리율을 고려해서 하향 조정 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 26년에도 AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 
시장은 성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 
때문이다. 26년 급격히 하락한 주가는 업황, 펀더멘탈을 설명하기에는 과한 움직임이라 
판단한다. 목표주가는 4,000,000원에서 3,200,000원으로 하향한다. 메모리 시장의 
업황에 대한 우려가 아니라 현재 주가와의 괴리율을 고려한 수준이다. 27년 EPS 
538,187원에 PER 6배를 적용하였다.

 SK하이닉스 (000660)

SK하이닉스 (000660)

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기, 모든 악재를 잠재운 실적 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 31.9% 증가한 104조 
6,150억원이다.  이전 전망 대비 증가한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 이전 
전망 대비 물량은 유사했지만 가격 상승폭은 예상보다 높았다. 
DRAM 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 35.4% 증가했다. B/G는 +11.5%, ASP는 
+28.2%로 추정했다. 3분기 DRAM 가격 상승률은 경쟁사 대비 높은 수준이다. Server 
DRAM 비중이 2분기 대비 높아진 것과 고객 믹스가 개선된 영향으로 추정한다. 
HBM4 비중은 30%를 상회한 것으로 추정한다. HBM이 3분기 가격에 미친 영향은 
제한적인 수준으로 판단한다.  
NAND 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 18.2% 증가했다. B/G는 -1%, ASP는 +26%로 
추정한다. 이전 전망치를 상회했다. SSD 비중은 2분기 75%에서 3분기에는 77%로 
상승했다.  
26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 37.6% 증가한 83.3조원이다. 이전 전망을 
상회한 수준이다. DRAM 영업이익은 지난 분기 대비 42.3% 증가한 65.1조원으로 
예상한다. 영업이익률은 전 분기 대비 4.0%p 상승한 83.0%로 추정한다. NAND 
영업이익은 전 분기 대비 23.1% 증가한 18.2조원으로 추정한다. 영업이익률은 전 
분기 대비 2.9%p 높아진 72.4%로 추정한다.

4 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 4분기, DRAM 물량 효과 
SK하이닉스의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 29.4% 증가한 135.3조원으로 
예상한다. 가격상승은 지속될 것으로 예상하지만 26년 3분기 대비 상승폭이 낮아질 
전망이다. 물량은 3분기 대비 25% 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 신규 라인 가동 영향이다. 
4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 34.1% 증가한 111.7조원으로 예상한다. DRAM 
90.9조원, NAND 20.7조원이다.  
DRAM 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 36.4% 증가한 107조원으로 예상한다. ASP는 
+15%, Bit Growth는 +25.0%로 가정하였다. HBM 출하는 지난 분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 예상하고, HBM4 비중은 60%대로 높아질 전망이다. 
26년 4분기 NAND 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 11.6% 증가할 것으로 예상한다. Bit 
Growth는 +5%로 전망하고, ASP는 +12%로 예상한다. SSD 비중은 소폭 개선될 
전망이다. SSD 내에서 eSSD 비중은 90% 수준이다.

IBKS RESEARCH │5 
공정 전환 속도는 예상보다 빠르게 진행 중: 1cb nm, 321L 
SK하이닉스는 1c nm 비중을 빠르게 확대하고 있다. Wafer 투입이 제한된 상황에서 
공급 확대를 위한 최선의 방법이기 때문이다. HBM 수요는 1b nm를 중심으로 대응할 
예정이다. 이와 관련된 물량도 꾸준히 증가하지만, 출하 Bit 기준으로 1c nm 비중이 
26년 1분기 3% 수준에서 26년 3분기에는 20%, 연말에는 33%까지 높아질 전망이다. 
HBM이 아닌 DRAM 물량이 크게 증가할 전망이다. 이를 통해서 높아진 DRAM 수요 
대응할 것으로 예상한다. 26년 출하 Bit Growth는 28.5%로 상향되었다. 
NAND 생산라인의 공정 전환은 High-End로 꾸준히 변화 중이다. 321L는 26년 
2분기에는 176L와 Cross over 되었다. 예상보다 1분기 빠른 속도다. 26년 3분기 321L 
비중은 50%이다. 26년말까지 321L를 64%까지 확대할 계획이다.

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
Check Point 
1. DRAM ASP의 차별화 
26년 3분기 DRAM ASP는 메이커별로 차별화가 크다. 26년 2분기 대비 상승폭은 마
이크론이 10% 후반, 삼성전자는 20% 초반으로 추정하고, SK하이닉스는 20% 후반이
다. LTA, HBM 등 다양한 변수가 있지만 SK하이닉스는 고객 비중 변화가 가장 큰 변
수라고 설명한다.  
2. HBM4 물량, 수율 문제 
26년 3분기에 가장 시끄러웠던 변수가 SK하이닉스의 HBM4 관련 품질 문제이다. 26
년 3분기 DRAM 매출과 영업이익으로는 문제가 있었다고 판단하기 어렵다. 26년 3분
기 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 30%를 상회한 것으로 추정되고 4분기 말에는 비중이 60%
까지 높아질 것으로 전망한다.   
3. HBM 8Hi 확대가 시장 규모 감소는 아니다. 
HBM4는 12Hi가 원래 제품 사양이다. HBM 공급이 부족해지면서 GPU를 좀 더 많이 
팔기 위해서 적용되는 HBM 용량을 줄이는 방향으로 제품 설계가 변경되는 중이다. 
2027년 하반기에는 8Hi 비중이 12Hi 비중을 상회할 것으로 예상한다. 하지만 이로 
인해서 HBM 전체 물량이 감소하는 것은 아닌 것으로 전망하고 있다.  
4. 투자와 생산은 다르다 
SK하이닉스는 용인팹을 공기를 앞당겨서 완공할 계획이다. 이로 인해서 공급량이 예
상 대비 커지는 것에 대한 우려가 있지만 건물 완공과 장비 반입에는 시차가 있고, 수
요를 고려하지 않은 장비 반입은 없을 것으로 밝혔다. 
5. 주주환원. 더 남아 있다 
공시를 통해서 26년 회계연도 기준 약 100조원의 주주환원 계획을 밝혔다. 배당을 30
조원 이상, 자사주 매입/소각을 60조원이상을 추진할 계획이다. 26년 4분기 중에 배
당은 14조원을 집행하고, 자사주는 20조원을 매입할 예정이다. 자사주 20조원 중에서 
10조원은 종업원 상여금 지급용이어서 주주환원에 포함되지 않는다.  
26년 4분기 실적 발표에서 추가적인 배당과 자사주 매입에 대한 계획을 발표할 것으
로 예상한다.

ASP 움직임은 메이커별로

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
펀더멘탈과 센티멘탈의 줄다리기 구간 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 시장은 
성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 때문이다. 
26년 급격히 하락한 주가는 업황, 펀더멘탈을 설명하기에는 과한 움직임이라 판단한다.  
목표주가는 4,000,000원에서 3,200,000원으로 하향한다. 메모리 시장의 업황에 대한 
우려가 아니라 현재 주가와의 괴리율을 고려한 수준이다. 2027년 EPS 538,187원에 PER 
6배를 적용하였다.  
아직 제대로 보급되지 않은 Agent AI가 확산되면 컴퓨팅 파워 필요성에 대한 인식도 
크게 바뀔 것으로 전망한다. 메타의 MUSE는 가입자당 8GB의 DRAM을 가정하고 
있다. Open AI, Google, Anthropic에서도 Agent Platform이 공개된다면 필요한 
DRAM은 폭발적으로 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 
메모리 수요에 대한 규모가 너무 커지는 것이 걱정이지 누가 살 것인지에 대한 것은 
걱정할 문제가 아니라 판단한다. 기존 AI 모델의 진화, 효율적 모델의 등장, Agent AI 
모델의 확대 모두 메모리 수요를 기하급수적으로 확장시키는 변수이다. 
투자 주체들의 자금난이 수요를 저해하는 변수는 아닌 것으로 판단한다. 대체 
투자자들은 지속적으로 증가할 것으로 전망하기 때문이다.  
메모리 업체들의 투자 계획은 수요에 대응하기 위한 투자일 뿐 공급과잉을 만들 
수준은 아닐 것으로 판단한다.

2 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Stronger Growth, Costlier Money
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The latest GDP report and annual revisions show a sturdier U.S. economy than previously
reported. According to the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA), real GDP grew at a 2.2%
annualized pace in Q2 compared to 1.5% previously estimated, and Q1 was revised
from 2.1% to 2.5%. Both 2024 and 2025 were also revised slightly higher. Most of the

first half of the year revision was in categories related to the Artificial Intelligence (AI)
buildout, but real consumer spending has also been revised slightly higher, accelerating
from 0.7% annualized growth in Q1 to 3.8% in Q2 despite surging energy prices and
slow hiring.
Spending in July and August remained much stronger than labor market conditions alone
would suggest, also averaging 3.7% annualized growth. Upside revisions to net interest

income and personal saving suggest households have benefited from more income than
was previously apparent, with personal saving revised higher back to 2023. The new data,
according to the BEA, show the January to July 2026 savings rate averaged 4.8% versus
3.3% prerevision, strengthening consumer spending underpinnings despite moderate
hiring, high energy prices and rising borrowing costs.
The BEA update also included inflation methodological changes related to categories

inflated by the AI boom—such as portfolio management fees and computer software and
accessories. These improved the inflation picture to some extent—August “core” PCE
inflation was reduced by 0.3 ppts to 3% YoY, bringing it closer to “core” consumer price
index inflation, which was 2.5% YoY and 2% on a three-month annualized basis. While still
above the 2% Federal Reserve (Fed) target, “core” PCE now appears stable at 3% year over
year since January rather than accelerating. These methodological changes are expected

to dampen pass-through of AI-related price pressures into “core” inflation and effects of
stock-market swings on inflation.
They also produced an encouraging improvement in recent PCE inflation momentum.
Three-month annualized “core” PCE inflation slowed from 4.3% at the peak of tariff
effects in February to 2.1% in August. For durable goods, this rate was down from 5.8%
in February to 1.5% in August, and, for services ex-energy and healthcare, it slowed from

4.2% to 2.2%, for example. Inflation is volatile on a three-month change basis, so it
remains to be seen whether this moderation continues without further Fed tightening.
For now, the trends in the San Francisco Fed’s cyclical versus acyclical inflation measures
diverged again in August. As expected, given softening wage growth, prices most sensitive
to the labor market have eased further. At 3% YoY, this inflation category is down from
8% in early 2023 and 4.6% two years ago is back to prepandemic levels and is likely

to ease more (Exhibit 1A). In contrast, supply bottlenecks, high energy prices and other
industry-specific factors have kept acyclical inflation on an uptrend. As shown in Exhibit
1B, the relentless increase in freight costs could prolong upside pressure on this category.
The diesel market has tightened in recent months, boosting its price by 70% YoY,
consistent with Brent oil at around $200 per barrel and large reverberations across
a wide swath of goods and services prices. A government-policy-related decline in

available truck drivers is also raising transportation costs more than expected. So are
tight ocean shipping capacity and longer/more expensive voyages due to the U.S./Iran
War. Supply-chain stress also increased as companies rushed to front-run potential new
tariff increases.
On the positive side for the inflation outlook, unit labor costs (ULC) are up modestly
as robust productivity growth has absorbed much of the wage growth, limiting a major
source of domestic inflation. Upside productivity growth revisions are likely to further

reduce ULC, which could help shift focus from energy and AI-related inflation to
eventual disinflationary effects once oil and refined fuel prices start to ease. Sustained
productivity gains would support growth and margins while limiting inflation and the
need for much higher interest rates.
Investment Implications
We favor selective Equity exposure
to companies with durable demand
and pricing power, while remaining
cautious about businesses most

exposed to fuel, freight, and
financing costs. We would also
gradually increase long-duration
exposure.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Surging nominal growth has boosted corporate revenues (up 13% YoY in Q2, according to the
BEA) while contained unit labor costs helped raise margins and profits more than expected.
With labor costs declining as a share of revenues, investment-related tax incentives and
rock-bottom net interest expense, pre-tax nonfinancial corporate profits as measured in GDP
accounts rose 21% YoY in Q2. Total corporate profits after-tax were up 18.2% YoY, according to

the BEA.
Surging AI-related imports and investment are keeping the business and profits cycle on
an uptrend both here and abroad. According to BofA Global Research, the global economic
cycle backdrop improved further in September, as reflected in a broad-based increase in its
Global Wave1 indicator. Broadening U.S. manufacturing strength, fueled by ongoing AI-driven
investment, a healthier orders backlog and low inventories suggest that the expansion has

room to run—a constructive environment for earnings. Typically, a Global Wave upswing is
consistent with rising earnings revisions ratios and equity price uptrends. According to the
Global Wave, global earnings growth expectations remain exceptionally strong.
Still, absent a resolution of war-related energy and transportation disruptions, the same
industrial strength intensifies inflation pressures. A prolonged supply shock raises the risk of
restrictive interest rates and weakening economic growth. In this context, the surge in the

10-year Treasury yield above 5% is starting to reflect more than optimism about U.S. growth
and strengthening productivity outlook. Oil’s tightest recent correlation with 10-year Treasury
yields in 35 years suggests a common energy-price thread behind rising yields in developed
countries.
Bond market spillovers from normalizing Japan yields and capital repatriation are another
common thread. Higher yields on foreign sovereign bonds such as in Japan—which has

escaped stagnation and deflation by intensely pursuing investment-led growth—weaken the
relative appeal of Treasurys. That distinction matters for portfolios. A yield increase driven
mainly by durable productivity gains would come with a stronger outlook for future income
and earnings. A yield increase driven by cost shocks and greater uncertainty raises financing
costs without the same benefit.
In sum, the economy remains more resilient than expected, but the bond market is also

charging more for an uncertain inflation outlook given untethered government debt and
cooling global demand for Treasurys spurred by geopolitical tensions and Japan capital
repatriation. The case for much further tightening becomes stronger if wage growth
strengthens, productivity lags, and unit labor costs reaccelerate, causing cyclical inflation to
also pick up steam. The case becomes weaker if that measure stays contained while fuel and
freight pressures recede or rising Treasury yields reduce asset prices and economic growth.

spike feed acyclical inflation.

4 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Yields, Yen and Iran: What Haunts the “House”
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
We recently highlighted the three-front challenge facing Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent:
engineering a stronger yen, containing the 10-year Treasury yield and securing a diplomatic
resolution to the conflict with Iran.

Bessent recently has delivered one of the year’s more memorable lines while discussing
Treasury policy and the yen: “I have asymmetric information. I am the house now.”2 Yet several
challenges remain: the 10-year Treasury yield is near levels last seen in the early 2000s, the
yen remains structurally weaker, and a phased agreement to reopen the Strait of Hormuz
appears remote. The resulting strain on energy markets keeps inflation risks elevated.

Put differently, oil prices, Treasury yields and the dollar-yen exchange rate are all trending
against Bessent’s objectives. Below, we examine how each variable is haunting his “house.”
Haunting the “House” is the Yen. Since the U.S. and Japan mounted a record $98.7B
coordinated intervention in late July after the yen weakened to a 40-year low near 164 per
dollar, the currency has appreciated roughly 4% against the dollar. The move was further
supported on September 18 when the BoJ raised rates by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%,

their highest level since the mid-1990s (Exhibit 2A). However, the split 7–2 vote and Governor
Ueda’s limited forward guidance leave the path of further tightening uncertain. The key
question is whether additional BoJ hikes can produce sustained appreciation in the yen, or
merely reinforce the temporary relief provided by the summer intervention.
Bessent, meanwhile, has repeatedly signaled his preference for a stronger yen. Partly because
yen stability is also a U.S. financing concern. Persistent currency weakness could prompt

Japan to support its currency by selling foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys—which would
be an unwelcome prospect as Washington partially relies on foreign capital flows to fund its
large fiscal deficits. That potential “haunts” the Treasury Secretary.  At precisely the moment
Washington needs foreign capital to finance its enormous fiscal deficits, it would be inopportune
for one of America’s largest creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities.

Haunting the “House” are Yields. The surge in yields runs counter to a White House intent
on lowering borrowing costs. The benchmark 10-year yield has broken above the 5% threshold
and climbed to levels not seen since 2007. At around 5.30%, it stands more than a full
percentage point above where it began the year (Exhibit 2B). The level is striking but so is the
speed of the move: Stronger-than-expected economic data, including a survey showing U.S.

2 September 8, 2026.
Investment Implications
While some key variables are
complicating the macro backdrop
(i.e., higher-for-longer yields/oil)
the combination of strong earnings
growth, robust consumer demand
(real final sales to private domestic
purchasers grew at 4.6% for Q2) and
ongoing capital investment suggests
that the fundamental backdrop for
U.S. Equities remains constructive.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
business activity expanded at the fastest pace in more than five years, helped trigger an 18
bps surge in the 10-year yield—among its largest daily moves in decades. The 5-year yield also
topped 5% for the first time since 2007, while the 30-year yield reached 5.63%, its highest
level since 2002.
Compounding the move is inflation, running above the Fed’s 2% target for 67 months,

large fiscal deficits and the prospect of continued growth in Treasury issuance. Against this
backdrop, Treasury bought back $4.1 billion of long-dated debt, versus a maximum of $6
billion. That effort has done little to arrest the selloff. A subsequent auction priced nearly $4
billion of 7-year notes at 5.085%, the highest yield for that maturity in 33 years.
All in, the MOVE index, a key gauge of Treasury market volatility, jumped from 78 to 95 on

Wednesday September 23 and then to 104 the following day, its highest level since the early
days of the Iran war (Exhibit 3A).
Haunting the “House” is the Iran conflict. Early expectations that military pressure and
economic sanctions would quickly bring Iran to the negotiating table have yet to materialize.
Iranian officials have signaled that a full reopening of the Strait of Hormuz remains unlikely
while sanctions stay in place. Although talks between Washington and Tehran are continuing,

conflicting signals and lingering skepticism from both sides have kept tensions elevated.
As a result, oil prices have remained elevated in a range even as some Middle East supply
disruptions began to ease.
Startling is that Brent crude is up more than 60% this year, but refined product prices
have risen even faster. Ongoing disruptions to energy flows through the Strait of Hormuz,
combined with Ukrainian strikes on Russian refining infrastructure have tightened global

supplies of crude oil and refined fuels. Saudi Arabia has restored some drone-disrupted flows,
providing roughly 3.5 million barrels a day of relief (roughly 3% of global consumption).
The squeeze is mostly acute in diesel markets, where inventories remain constrained and
prices continue to climb (Exhibit 3B). U.S. diesel prices recently surpassed $6.50 per gallon,
the highest level on record, just as Russia has extended restrictions on diesel exports into

October. Against this backdrop, proposals to ban U.S. fuel exports risks worsening the
imbalance. According to S&P Global, an export ban could reduce refinery runs by nearly 2
million barrels per day and significantly cut gasoline production, potentially driving gasoline
and jet fuel prices even higher. As long as tensions persist, the oil impulse remains a key
impediment to structurally lower inflation.
Investment implications. Treasury Secretary Bessent increasingly sits at the intersection of

monetary policy, fiscal policy, foreign policy and national security. We remain constructive on
the U.S. economy and overweight U.S. Equities but are watching as these fronts challenge the
narrative. There is a great deal riding on the “house” versus the key macro variables of today.

A) Bond and Equity Market Volatility Diverge. B) Pain at the Pump: Diesel Prices Surge Ahead of Gasoline.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
When It Comes to Attracting Foreign Capital, the U.S. Remains Exceptional
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
There may be no better vote of confidence in a country than the willingness of
foreigners to put their money there. And by that measurement, the U.S. remains the
exception—both portfolio and FDI inflows are currently running at record highs based

on the latest data. This dynamic is one key reason why we remain constructive on U.S.
assets over the longer term.
Notwithstanding all the handwringing about America’s fiscal deficits, tariffs and rising
government debt, global investors sunk some $942B in net purchases of U.S. Equities in
the 12 months to July, the highest rolling 12-month total on record. In Q2, net purchase
of U.S. Equities soared to $426B, up 62% from the same period a year ago (Exhibit 4A).

By country, flows were down from China—not surprisingly—but strong from Taiwan and
South Korea, who have taken to recycling their massive AI-generated trade surplus back
into the U.S. In the end, strong portfolio inflows suggest that international investors
remain willing to increase their exposure to the world's largest capital market—that’s
bullish for U.S. assets.
Meanwhile, Exhibit 4B highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be

inside the U.S., with FDI inflows totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year.
That is a staggering 103% jump from a year ago and means FDI inflows are on track for
a record year, estimated at $480B for 2026.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Two, within the $32 trillion-plus economy with extraordinarily consumer

purchasing power, foreign firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment
than just trade (exports). Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers,
semiconductors, power generation, etc., has created numerous potential investment
opportunities for many foreign firms that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging
capex cycle. Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly
developed technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.

In the end, double-barreled capital inflows speak to the absolute and relative
attractiveness of the U.S. economy and capital markets. Stay invested in America—the
rest of the world is.
Exhibit 4: U.S. Exceptionalism: Record Inflows of Portfolio and FDI Inflows.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

ICE BofA MOVE Index is a market-implied measure of U.S. bond market volatility.
Consumer Price Index measures the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a market basket of consumer goods and services.
Baltic Dry Index is a daily shipping and trade index published by the Baltic Exchange in London that measures the cost of moving raw materials like iron ore, coal, and grain by sea.
Volatility Index (VIX) is a real-time market indicator that measures the stock market's expected volatility over the next 30 days.

Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IN FRANCE: DOUBLE-DIGIT GROWTH IN BUSINESS CREATIONS,
LOWER INCREASE IN BANKRUPTCIES, AND STABLE NON-PERFORMING LOANS – PARTIAL UPDATE WITH
MONTHLY DATA AS OF SEPTEMBER 2026
ECO CHARTS | BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IS A NEW PUBLICATION AIMED AT PROVIDING AN OVERVIEW OF THE DYNAMICS
OF BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IN FRANCE, AS WELL AS THERISKS ASSOCIATED WITH THE LATTER. WE WILL PUBLISH

REGULAR UPDATES TO THIS PUBLICATION.

### Page 2

Double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans
2ECOCHARTSECONOMIC STUDIES
• Business creations rose by 16% y/y as of Q3 2026, marking an acceleration compared with the growth seen in 2025 (5.3% y/y).
• This increase was driven by sectors associated with new information and communication technologies, where the trend is gaining
momentum.
• Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 1.7% y/y as of Q3 2026, according to the data from the Banque de France (vs. 3.6% y/y in

2025). After a more pronounced rise in the first quarter (8% y/y), this increase has since slowed. This moderation could continue, as,
according to Altares, payment delays have decreased to an average of 12.3 days in the second quarter of 2026 (14.1 days a year
earlier), compared with the European average of 13.1 days.
• The rise in business bankruptcies is most pronounced in the services sector, which has simultaneously seen an increase in business

creations. This trend is clear in the retail sector, with a 26% y/y increase in business creations as of Q3 2026, while business
bankruptcies are up 3% y/y.
• In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been declining since Q2 2026.
• The level of the average ratio of non-performing loans is moderately high and has remained stable for the past year (4.0% of total
loans and advances in Q2 2026). More cyclical than in other sectors, the ratio of non-performing loans in construction reached its

highest level since 2019, at 9.5%.
• The share of underperforming loans, but not yet non-performing (in Stage 2, according to IFRS 9 terminology), which provides an
indication of the future trend in non-performing loans, is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all-time high (16.1%).
• Despite a significant rise since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies — which allows them to be weighted by a proxy for
company size — remains in September, on average, below its Q1 2025 peak (0.54 compared with 0.61, respectively). The economic

weight of bankrupted small businesses decreased but still stands at a high level (2.03).
For more information, please refer to our publication: Chart of the week | French corporates: peaks in creations put peaks in bankruptcies into
perspective. July 22, 2026 – Economic Research – BNP Paribas

Document 326: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ November 9-13

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: November 9-13
Date:    3 hours ago
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: November 9-13

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
November 9-13
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 7 Nov 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

The S&P 500 fell 2.7% last week, and the Nasdaq dropped 4.9%, both
posting their fourth straight week of losses. Thursday’s 4.4% plunge in the
S&P 500, its worst day since April 2025, came as investors weighed what
the Democratic sweep of Congress means for the AI data center buildout.
Both indexes rebounded Friday on strong jobs data, with the S&P 500 up
0.3% and the Nasdaq up 1.4%.
October’s CPI (Tue) and PPI (Fri) headline this week’s data calendar. Ten

Fed officials are scheduled to speak, and the UK and Eurozone report Q3
GDP. Wednesday’s Veterans Day holiday closes the bond market. Here’s
more:
(1) Earnings season. So far, 88% of S&P 500 companies have reported
their Q3 results. So 60 companies still have to report, and 13 will do so
this week. The "Earnings Squiggles" for 2026, 2027, and 2028 are likely to
rise even further over the remainder of this year (chart). Currently, industry
analysts' consensus is that S&P 500 companies’ aggregate EPS will be

$366.60 this year, $426.30 next year, and $495.10 in 2028. In the past,
Earnings Squiggles tended to fall because analysts were too optimistic.
This time, they haven't been optimistic enough!
(2) Fedspeak. Ten Fed officials are scheduled to speak this week
(Goolsbee, Barkin, Waller, Logan, Schmid, Paulson, Hammack, Daly,
Williams, and Jefferson). These are their first remarks after the
Democratic sweep. These Fed heads are likely hawkish on balance,

especially after Friday's strong employment report.

### Page 4

In early October, the federal funds rate (FFR) futures implied 3.3 rate
hikes over the next 12 months and 1.7 over the next six. Since then, those
projections have fallen to 1.9 and 1.4 (chart). Friday’s employment report
lifted the odds of a December rate hike to 78%. We think the FOMC still
needs another 50bps of increases to reach a neutral FFR.
San Francisco Fed President Mary Daly told CNBC on Friday that “the job
market remains stable and solid, but not booming,” with real wages falling

as pay lags inflation. She expects the Fed “will have to tighten policy
further.”
(3) Inflation. October’s CPI and PPI reports will be released on Tuesday
and Friday, respectively. The Cleveland Fed’s Inflation Nowcasting model
projects that the headline CPI rose 0.24% m/m and the core CPI 0.21%
m/m. That would hold headline CPI inflation at 3.7% y/y and ease core to
2.5% from 2.6% (chart). October would mark the 68th consecutive month
of CPI inflation above 2.0% y/y. The model sees both accelerating in

November, to 3.9% and 2.8% y/y.
The Nowcast also suggests that October’s PCED inflation will remain
firmly above the Fed’s 2.0% target, with the headline and core PCED
inflation rates projected at 3.8% y/y and 3.2% y/y, respectively. Both are
expected to heat up a bit in November.

### Page 5

October's PPI inflation is likely to be hotter than September's headline and
core readings of 6.3% and 5.0% (chart).
One of the more troublesome components of the PPI recently has been
transportation & warehousing services, which jumped 16.0% y/y in
September (chart). Soaring diesel prices are fueling inflation.

### Page 6

(4) ADP & unemployment claims. Payrolls rose 165,000 in October, well
above the 65,000 consensus. The unemployment rate fell to 4.1% from
4.2%. ADP’s weekly employment reading (Tue) follows a four-week
average of 16,750 for the week ended October 10, down from 17,500
(chart). ADP’s monthly report showed private payrolls up 86,000 in
October, beating the 67,000 consensus.
Initial jobless claims (Thu) follow last week's 189,000, with the four-week
average falling to 194,750, the lowest since 1969 (chart). Continuing

claims were 1,718,000 for the week ended October 23, with a four-week
average of 1,709,000.

### Page 7

(5) Global. The UK’s Q3 GDP (Thu) rose at an estimated 0.7% q/q pace,
up from 0.5% in Q2, when real GDP was up 1.4% y/y (chart).
The Eurozone’s updated flash estimate of Q3 GDP (Fri) follows the initial
0.4% q/q reading. Real GDP rose 1.3% y/y in Q3, with Spain leading the
major economies at 2.7% and France down 0.1% (chart).

### Page 8

clause.

Document 340: FX Daily_ Don’t mess with the dollar near term

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Daily_ Don’t mess with the dollar near term.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

Hot US inﬂation and the prospect of a more aggressive
response from the Fed are the themes dominating FX markets
currently. Another dose of ﬁrm US PPI today and some more
hawkish Fed rhetoric are set to keep the dollar very supported
into next week

Chris Turner, Frantisek Taborsky and Francesco Pesole
October US PPI data today should keep short-dated yields and the dollar bid
Please see ING's FX Outlook 2027: Fat tails, slim ranges for
insights into key themes next year.
USD: Do not stand in the way of the
dollar
This week has seen a powerful, macro-driven rally in the dollar.
Short-dated US yields are close to 40bp higher since Tuesday
morning, when the Federal Reserve's Christopher Waller mused
over the need for a 50bp hike in December. The market has since

### Page 2

adopted the view that back-to-back December and January hikes
look likely, while extending the full Fed adjustment from here to a
full 85bp of tightening. That's beginning to look a lot more like a
cycle than the kind of modest adjustment we recently presented in
our house baseline view. Notably, a few Fed members are starting
to describe conditions more as a demand than a supply shock.
It is hard to see what is going to change this hawkish Fed/dollar-

positive mindset in the next few weeks. The Fed centrists might try
to emphasise the need for a gradual adjustment in policy, but it is
probably only a big US tech sell-oﬀ that can put a dent in short-
dated US yields now.
In terms of the FX reaction, the low-yielding Swiss franc and the yen
have some of the highest correlations with US rates and are under
pressure. 0.8400 is a really important resistance area for USD/CHF
and will probably help determine whether the dollar needs to take

another big leg higher over the coming weeks. USD/JPY is already
trading through the psychological level at 160, and we would not
expect any intervention yet given the macro nature of the broadly
stronger dollar. As we said on Wednesday, we expect to see dollar
strength more expressed against activity currencies, where
something like the low-yielding Swedish krona looks vulnerable.
For today, the October US PPI looks likely to keep the narrative of

hot US inﬂation alive. Expect scrutiny into the components of PPI,
such as healthcare, airfares and software, that feed into the Fed's
preferred PCE inﬂation data – the next release of which is on 25
November. Were that core PCE to rise 0.3% month-on-month for the
third month in a row, expectations of a 50bp December hike would
grow.
DXY has broken to a new high for the year and looks like it can push
on to the next resistance level at 104.60. That's about a 1.5%

advance from current levels and would be consistent with EUR/USD
trading around 1.0880.
Chris Turner
EUR: ECB struggles to outhawk the Fed

### Page 3

European Central Bank rhetoric ﬁrmly points to the need for a rate
hike in December. The problem for EUR/USD is that the market sees
the potential for a prolonged tightening cycle as more credible in
the US than in the eurozone. You don't hear many ECB speakers
talking of a demand shock in Europe. Short-dated EUR/USD swap
diﬀerentials have moved out to the widest levels of the year and are
clearly weighing on the pair.
Given the run of expected US inﬂation data over the coming weeks,

we struggle to see what can take the pressure oﬀ EUR/USD. A break
below 1.10 is not a given, but that level is certainly at risk into year-
end. EUR/USD bulls will have to be hoping for either an AI crash, the
ECB sounding far more hawkish or perhaps some major
geopolitical breakthrough in Iran or Ukraine.
In focus today will be the second release of third-quarter eurozone
GDP data. This printed a respectable 0.4% quarter-on-quarter
previously. And there are also several ECB speakers – all likely to

point to a December rate hike.
Chris Turner
CEE: The pressure on FX is far from over
Friday will bring more data to the CEE region than the relatively
quiet days we have seen this week. This morning, Q3 GDP ﬁgures
for Romania and Poland will be released. While the former is
expected to show some acceleration, the latter is likely to reﬂect a
slowdown. Poland will also release its ﬁnal inﬂation reading for
October, expected at 3.5%, and on Monday, we'll receive the core

inﬂation ﬁgure, which we estimate has slowed to 2.8%.
In Turkey, inﬂation expectations for November will be published.
Yesterday, the Central Bank of Turkey revised its inﬂation forecast
for next year from 15% to 21%, bringing it closer to the current
market expectation of 23% but also opening the door to a
December rate cut. The Czech National Bank will release the
minutes from last week’s meeting, during which it raised interest
rates by 25bp to 4.00%. However, the 4-3 vote split sent a dovish

The CEE market remains under pressure. Yesterday’s surge in
energy prices and new lows in the EUR/USD pair are pushing
regional FX to new lows. In line with our bearish bias, we believe this
is far from over. EUR/HUF could test 366, but we believe the 368-370
range remains an area for new sellers to enter, eﬀectively capping
the pair. Meanwhile, the National Bank of Hungary is expected to

take a ﬁrm stance next week and avoid rushing into rate cuts
despite low inﬂation, which could support the forint, given that one
to two rate cuts are currently priced in.
EUR/CZK reached new local highs yesterday, nearing 24.400. We
believe this should mark the peak, unless the CNB minutes reveal a
more dovish tone than what we observed last week.
Frantisek Taborsky
BRL: Lula trying to take some ﬁscal
action
The Brazilian real has slumped since incumbent President Luiz

Inácio Lula da Silva beat Flavio Bolsonaro in the run-oﬀ vote last
month. At the heart of the concern is the ﬁscal side, where Lula's
pre-election proposals have only added to concern about Brazil's
ﬁscal position. Over the last few days, however, Lula's government
has started to propose some ﬁscal consolidation for the 2027
budget, and we presume more is coming.
While Brazil's ﬁscal story is vulnerable, its monetary story is
supportive for the real. High real interest rates and an independent

central bank make the Brazilian real an expensive sell. We look for a
recovery in 2027. Please see the Latam section in our 2027 FX
Outlook for more.
Chris Turner

Document 376: NZ総選挙は与党連合が辛うじて過半数確保も、政権の行方は流動的 ～政権の枠組み次第では金融政策の目標が見直される可能性、ＮＺ_22dd47d2

Source PDF: all report pdf/NZ総選挙は与党連合が辛うじて過半数確保も、政権の行方は流動的 ～政権の枠組み次第では金融政策の目標が見直される可能性、ＮＺ_22dd47d2.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年 11 月 9 日

1/5 
NZ 総選挙は与党連合が辛うじて過半数確保も、政権の行方は流動的 
～政権の枠組み次第では金融政策の目標が見直される可能性、ＮＺドル相場にも不透明感～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ニュージーランドでは、11 月７日に議会総選挙が実施され、暫定結果では国民党を中心とす
る与党３党が合計67 議席を獲得し、過半数をわずか１議席上回った。ただし、約 40 万票の
特別票が未集計であり、11 月27 日の公式結果の公表まで政権の行方は不透明な状況が続く。
今回の選挙では、物価高による生活費負担の増大や雇用環境の悪化、景気回復の遅れなどを
背景に、既存の二大政党への支持が低下し、中小政党が存在感を強めた。 
○ ニュージーランド準備銀行（RBNZ）は 10 月の定例会合で、市場予想に反して政策金利を2.75％
に据え置いた。インフレ率が目標レンジを上回る一方、雇用環境の悪化や景気減速への懸念
が強まっており、金融政策の判断は難しさを増している。また、総選挙後に労働党を中心と
する政権が成立した場合には、金融政策の目標に雇用の最大化を再び加える「デュアル・マ
ンデート」が復活する可能性がある。ただし、政策目標の変更が直ちに金融緩和につながる
とは限らず、中銀の独立性を維持した政策判断が重要となる。 
○ 先行きは、12 月９日の次回会合で追加利上げが検討される可能性があるものの、その判断は
インフレ圧力の持続性や景気・雇用動向に左右されよう。ＮＺドル相場は、RBNZ の追加利上
げ観測が下支え要因となる一方、政権の枠組みを巡る不透明感や豪州との金利差拡大などが
重石となり、当面は上値の重い展開が続くと見込まれる。さらに、景気の下振れや世界的な
リスク回避姿勢の強まりによっては、ＮＺドル安が一段と進む可能性にも注意が必要である。

【総選挙は与党連合が過半数を暫定的に確保も、政権の行方は依然として流動的】 
ニュージーランドでは、11 月７日に議会（代議院：一院制）の総選挙が実施された。同国では、
小選挙区比例代表併用制（ＭＭＰ）が採用されており、原則として政党票の得票率に応じて議席が
配分される一方、単独政党が過半数を獲得することは難しく、複数政党による連立政権が形成され
てきた。今回の総選挙を巡っては、2023 年に発足したラクソン首相率いる国民党、ＡＣＴ党、ＮＺ
ファースト党の中道右派連立政権が継続するか、ヒプキンス党首率いる最大野党・労働党を中心と
する政権への交代が実現するかが焦点となった。

### Page 2

2/5 
選挙管理委員会が公表した暫定集計によれば、政党票の得票率は労働党が 29.36％、国民党が
29.21％と、労働党がわずかに上回った。
一方で、暫定的な議席配分では、国民党
が 42 議席、労働党が36 議席となったほ
か、緑の党が 16 議席、ＡＣＴ党が 13 議
席、ＮＺファースト党が12 議席、新興の
オポチュニティ党が７議席、マオリ党が
５議席となった（図１）。国民党、ＡＣＴ
党、ＮＺファースト党の与党３党は合計
67 議席を獲得する計算となり、暫定的な
総議席数131 議席における過半数（66 議
席）をわずか１議席上回った。ただし、
現時点では在外投票をはじめとする特別票（約 40 万票）が 未集計であり、11 月27 日の公式結果の
発表まで、最終的な議席配分は確定しない。このため、与党連合による政権維持が確実になった訳
ではなく、各党間の連立協議や新興政党の対応も含め、政治情勢の不透明感が続くであろう。 
今回の選挙では、国民党が政党票の得票率に基づく配分議席を上回る 42 小選挙区を獲得したた
め、７つの超過議席が発生した。マオリ党についても４つの超過議席が生じ、議会の総議席数は定
数の 120 議席から 131 議席に大幅に拡大する見通しとなっている。こうした選挙制度上の特徴が、
国民党の政党票が労働党を下回ったにもかかわらず、与党連合が議席数で過半数を確保する要因と
なっている。一方、今回初めて議席獲得に必要な得票率５％の壁を超えたオポチュニティ党は、最
終結果次第で連立協議の行方を左右する存在となる可能性がある。また、2023 年の前回総選挙で
は、特別票の集計により国民党が暫定結果から２議席を失うなど議席配分に変化が生じており、今
回も公式結果の公表を待つ必要がある。

【物価高と雇用環境の悪化が有権者の不満を強め、二大政党離れが進む結果に】 
今回の総選挙では、有権者の投票行動
を左右した重要な要因として、物価高に
よる生活費負担の増大と景気回復の遅れ
が挙げられる。同国経済は、2024 年から
2025 年にかけて、高金利や内需低迷を背
景に厳しい状況に直面した。2025 年末か
ら 2026 年初めにかけて景気に持ち直し
の動きが確認されたものの、家計消費や
住宅投資の回復は鈍く、輸出関連産業と
内需関連産業の間で景況感の乖離が続い
ている。加えて、2026 年に入って以降は、
図 1 政党別獲得議席数(暫定集計ベース)

（出所）NZ 選管より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成, 太字が現与党

### Page 3

3/5 
イラン情勢の悪化による原油価格の上昇が家計の実質購買力を圧迫する要因となった。こうしたな
か、７-９月のインフレ率は前年比＋3.7％と、前期（同＋4.1％）から鈍化したものの、RBNZ の目
標レンジ（１～３％）を上回っている（図２）。さらに、７-９月の失業率は 5.7％と約 11 年ぶりの
高水準となるなど、家計を取り巻く環境は厳しさを増している。 
こうした経済環境は、有権者の政治に対する評価に影響を与えたと考えられる。今回の暫定結果
では、国民党と労働党の得票率の合計が６割を下回った一方、緑の党やＡＣＴ党、ＮＺファースト
党、オポチュニティ党などが支持を広げた。これは、既存政党の経済運営に対する不満に加え、有
権者の政策志向が多様化していることを示唆している。とはいえ、各党の支持拡大の背景は一様で
はなく、経済・財政政策の違いに加えて、環境問題、移民政策、先住民マオリの権利なども投票判
断に影響したとみられる。また、選挙管理委員会の推計による投票率は 74.6％と、前回の 78.2％
を下回った。今回の結果は、与党に対する評価だけでなく、野党を含む既存の二大政党に対する有
権者の評価が分かれたことを反映したものと捉えられる。

【RBNZは10 月会合で予想外の金利据え置き、インフレと景気減速リスクの間で難しい判断に直面】 
RBNZ による金融政策も総選挙の重要な争点のひとつとなった。RBNZ は、2026 年７月と９月の定
例会合において、それぞれ 25bp の利上げを決定し、政策金利であるオフィシャルキャッシュレー
ト（ＯＣＲ）を 2.75％まで引き上げてき
た。しかし、10 月の定例会合では、事前
には幅広く 25bp の追加利上げが予想さ
れていたにもかかわらず、政策金利を据
え置く決定を行った（注１）（図３）。こ
の決定を巡っては、７人の金融政策委員
のうち、ブレマン総裁ら４人が据え置き
を支持する一方、残る３人が 25bp の利上
げを主張する僅差の採決となった。これ
は、RBNZ の内部においても、景気への影
響を見極めるかを巡って判断が分かれて
いることを示唆している。 
RBNZ が追加利上げを見送った背景には、物価上昇圧力が続く一方、その持続性について慎重に判
断する必要があるとの認識がある。10 月の定例会合後に発表された声明文では、７-９月のインフ
レ率が事前予想を下回り、賃金上昇率も抑制されており、中期的なインフレ期待も安定しているこ
とを指摘した。さらに、７月と９月に実施した利上げの影響を見極める必要があるとの見方が示さ
れた。一方、基調的なインフレ率は目標レンジの上限付近まで高まり、価格上昇が幅広い品目に広

注１ 10 月28 日付レポート「RBNZ の予想外の利上げ見送りで、NZ ドル相場は一段と弱含むか」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666951.html） 
図 3 政策金利(OCR)の推移

（出所）LSEG より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(%)

### Page 4

4/5 
がっていることに警戒感を示した。加えて、イラン情勢の悪化による原油価格の高止まりやＮＺド
ル安が輸入物価を押し上げることから、10-12 月のインフレ率は前年同期比＋4.4％に再加速する
と予想している。このため、RBNZ は実際の政策判断については今後の経済・物価動向を見極める姿
勢を示した。

【総選挙後の政権の枠組み次第では金融政策の目標が見直される可能性も、RBNZの独立性が重要に】 
総選挙後の金融政策を考えるうえで重要なのが、RBNZ の政策目標を巡る与野党間の考え方の違
いであろう。国民党を中心とする現政権は、2023 年に金融政策の目標から最大限持続可能な雇用の
達成を削除し、物価安定に重点を置く政策枠組みへと変更した。一方で、労働党は今回の総選挙の
公約に、物価安定と最大限持続可能な雇用の達成を併せて重視する「デュアル・マンデート」の復
活を掲げた。このため、新政権の構成次第では、RBNZ の政策運営を巡る制度的な枠組みが再び見直
される可能性がある。 
とはいえ、政策目標の見直しが直ちに利下げや金融緩和につながるとは限らない。仮に労働党を
中心とする政権が成立し、最大限持続可能な雇用の達成という目標が復活した場合においても、
RBNZ には物価安定を維持する責務が引き続き課される。インフレ率が目標レンジを上回るなか、雇
用環境の悪化を考慮しながらも、物価上昇圧力の持続性を慎重に見極める必要がある。また、政権
交代に伴う財政政策の変更が需要やインフレ期待に与える影響によっては、政策金利の引き下げ余
地が制約される可能性もある。一方、国民党を中心とする連立政権が継続する場合、財政規律の維
持によるインフレ抑制効果が期待されるものの、歳出抑制が家計消費や雇用の回復を鈍らせる可能
性もある。いずれの政権が成立しても、金融政策は政府の意向ではなく、中銀が経済・物価見通し
に基づいて独立して判断するという基本原則が重要となる。 
10 月会合での金利据え置き決定を巡っては、総選挙直前のタイミングであったため、その政治的
な影響にも関心が集まった。ブレマン総裁は定例会合後の記者会見において、総選挙の日程は金融
政策の判断に影響していないと説明し、政策目標の達成に必要な経済情勢の評価を優先していると
強調した。このため、金利据え置きの決定を選挙対策や政治的配慮によるものとみなすことは適切
ではない。金融政策委員会の見解が僅差で分かれたことそのものが、インフレ抑制と景気への配慮
の間で政策判断が難しくなっていることを示唆している。

【RBNZは12 月会合で追加利上げを再検討か、NZドル相場は政治情勢と金利見通しに左右される】 
先行きの金融政策については、12 月９日に予定される次回会合が重要な節目となる。RBNZ は 10
月会合において、今後公表される経済指標が政策判断を左右する可能性を指摘しており、追加利上
げの選択肢を維持している。金融市場の見通しも、12 月会合での 25bp の利上げを見込む向きがあ
り、2027 年にかけて政策金利が 3.25～4.00％程度まで引き上げられるとの予想が示されている。
とはいえ、足元のインフレ圧力が一時的な供給要因にとどまる場合には追加利上げを急ぐ必要性が
低下する一方で、輸入物価の上昇が賃金やサービス価格に波及する場合には、さらなる金融引き締
めが必要となる可能性がある。

### Page 5

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
5/5 
金融市場においては、ＮＺドル相場に下押し圧力がかかる動きがみられる。また、10 月会合での
予想外の金利据え置きを受けて、ＮＺド
ルは対米ドルで急落した（図４）。先行き
のＮＺドル相場については、RBNZ の追加
利上げ観測が下支え要因となる一方、政
権の枠組みを巡る不透明感が上値を抑
える可能性がある。国民党を中心とする
与党連合が最終的に過半数を確保でき
ない場合には、連立協議の長期化や政策
運営の不透明感が意識されやすくなる。
さらに、オーストラリア準備銀行（ＲＢ
Ａ）が 11 月会合で政策金利を 4.85％に
引き上げたことで、両国の政策金利差が拡大していることも、対豪ドルでＮＺドルの上値を抑える
要因となり得る（注２）。 
短期的にはＮＺドル相場の上値が重い展開が続くと見込まれる。先行きについては、インフレ圧
力が持続して RBNZ による利上げ観測が強まれば、ＮＺドルが下支えされる可能性はある。政治情
勢の安定化によって不確実性が低下した場合には、これまでの調整の反動も重なり、ＮＺドル相場
の持ち直しにつながることも考えられる。一方で、雇用環境が一段と悪化するとともに、景気が下
振れして追加利上げ観測が後退すれば、ＮＺドル安が一段と進む可能性もある。加えて、イラン情
勢の悪化に伴う原油高など、世界的なリスク回避姿勢が強まる場合には、国内金利の上昇にもかか
わらずＮＺドル安が進む可能性がある点には注意が必要である。

Document 393: September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-13).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 18
Status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
September CPI: Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
16:08 October 28 2026 Share
September Monthly CPI: Headline 0.8%mth/4.3%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.3.%mth/3.6%yr.
Market services ex volatile 1.6%mth/4.0%yr. September Quarter CPI: Headline
1.3%qtr/3.9%yr; Trimmed Mean 1.0%qtr/3.7%yr.
The CPI rose 0.8% in the September month, stronger than Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the

market’s expectation of 0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr, The seasonally
adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting some positive seasonality in the month.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: automotive fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday
travel & accommodation (8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation (7.2%mth); and
property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–
3.1%mth); household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and market expectations
with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr (revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised

pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this series.
Headline CPI lifted 1.3% in the September quarter compared to an expectation of 1.3% by Westpac
and 1.2% by the market. This saw the annual pace hold at 3.9%yr.
The RBA’s key underlying inflation measure, the quarterly TM, lifted 1.0% vs an expectation of
0.9% by Westpac and market estimate of 1.0%. The annual pace lifted from 3.6%yr to 3.7%yr
with the six-month annualised pace accelerating to 3.7%yr from 3.4%yr.

Overall, the estimates come in broadly as expected so we see minimal risk to our current December
quarter forecast of 1.3%qtr for the headline CPI. Softer housing inflation does present a very modest
downside risk to our 0.9%qtr for the TM but there are offsetting gains elsewhere.
The September CPI update revealed underlying inflationary pressures continuing even while a
moderation in housing inflation (dwellings in particular) is unfolding slightly faster than we had

anticipated. As such, the near-term risk for inflation remains to the upside. The RBA will remain alert
to these near-term inflationary risks and as so, we hold to the view that the RBA will raise rates again
at the November meeting.

### Page 3

Month of September CPI
The monthly CPI rose 0.8%mth in September, above both our expectation of a 0.6%mth increase and
the market forecast of 0.7%mth. This lifted the annual pace by 0.3ppts to 4.3%yr. On a seasonally
adjusted basis, the CPI increased 0.6%mth.
The key contributors to the monthly increase were: automotive fuel (9.4%mth, +0.3ppts); and holiday
travel & accommodation (7.6%mth, +0.5ppts), alongside a smaller contribution from the annual reset in

property rates (5.5%mth, +0.1ppt). There was no single major offset. Instead, several components
across household furnishings and clothing & footwear recorded modest declines, together subtracting a
little over 0.1ppt from the monthly result.
The monthly trimmed mean (TM) rose 0.3%mth, in line with both our and the market’s expectations.
The annual pace edged up to 3.6%yr, although the August outcome was revised down to 3.5%yr.
Underlying inflation has remained broadly stuck in the 3.5–3.6%yr range since May. On a 3m/3m basis,

the monthly TM was 1.1%.

### Page 4

Market services excluding volatile items and holiday travel were flat in the month, with annual inflation
easing to 3.8%yr, its softest pace since November 2025. On a seasonally adjusted basis, monthly
inflation moderated across most components in this measure, with telecommunications services, rents
and other financial services the key exceptions. However, all but two components are still recording
annual price growth above 3%, suggesting that disinflation across market services remains slow and

relatively narrow. The persistence of these underlying price pressures is likely to remain a concern for
the RBA.

### Page 5

Housing inflation was notably softer in the month, rising 0.3%mth to be 5.2% higher over the year.
Rents increased 0.2%mth, slightly below our expectation for a 0.3%mth rise. The biannual indexation
of Commonwealth Rent Assistance occurred late in the month, weighing on the September outcome,
with the remainder of the impact expected to flow through in October. The ABS noted that rents would
have risen 0.3%mth in the absence of the measure, while on a seasonally adjusted basis rents

increased 0.4%mth.
New dwelling inflation was flat in the month, the softest reading since May 2025. Monthly inflation has
now slowed in each successive month since May 2026, suggesting homebuilders’ pricing power is
becoming increasingly constrained. The ABS noted that some project home builders raised base prices
this month only to be offset by some builders increasing discounts and promotional offers in response
to slowing demand. The annual pace eased to 4.7%yr. Dwelling repair costs were also a downside

surprise, falling 0.1%mth, their first decline since February 2025, although prices remain 3.4% higher
over the year. Some of this softness was offset by a firmer-than-expected increase in property rates
and charges, which rose 5.6%mth.
Recently, we have also focused on audio, visual and computing given the potential for spillovers from
strong global demand for semiconductors and other computing resources associated with the AI build
out. Prices for both equipment and services came in weaker than August, with equipment prices falling

1.1%mth, while services rose 0.3%mth. Despite this, annual price growth remains relatively high for
these categories, running at 4.6% and 4.9% respectively. Some of this pressure might also be showing

### Page 6

through in gaming, toys and hobbies, which is running at 5.2%mth/15.5%yr.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second consecutive month. The share of the basket
running above 3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, meanwhile the share below 2%yr lifted from 27% to 32%.
Our replication of the San Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum served as a good guide to the
quarterly TM. With the full monthly reads in for the September quarter in, this measure does signal
some persistence/upside pressures for the quarterly TM going into the coming quarters. It is worth
cautioning that this measure will be impacted as the ABS refines its seasonal adjustment process for
the monthly indexes.

Compared to our expectations, the key surprises were:
Upside surprises
Downside surprises
Fruit & vegetables was the key upside surprise, 0.6%mth against our expectations for 3.7%mth fall.
Holiday travel & accommodation also surprised to the upside, following a run of downside surprises
since early this year. It rose 7.6%mth against our expectations for a 4.4%mth lift. 
Automotive fuel rose 9.4%mth against expectations of an 8.3%mth lift.

### Page 7

September Quarter CPI
Clothing & footwear was the key downside surprise with prices falling 2.7%mth against our
expectations for a 0.2%mth fall. The misses were broad-based: garments (–2.3%mth vs –2.0%mth);
footwear (–2.5%mth vs 1.8%mth); and accessories & clothing services (–4.1%mth vs 2.8%mth).
Household textiles declined 6.6%mth against our expectations for a 3.2%mth fall. Furniture &
furnishings rose by less-than-expected 0.4%mth vs 1.6%mth. Household appliances, utensils and

tools also came in softer, –3.2%mth vs –2.4%mth.
Dairy prices fell 1.2%mth against our expectations for a 0.1%mth lift.

### Page 8

The September quarter CPI printed spot on our expectation of 1.3%. The key factor driving this
increase was the slightly stronger surge in auto fuel (7.8%, +0.27ppt) and holiday travel &
accommodation (2.7%qtr, +0.16ppt) being offset by a slightly softer than expected increase in housing
(1.1%qtr, +0.24ppt) with rents and dwellings both increasing 1.0% in the quarter and utilities lifting
1.2%. Food was also slightly stronger than expected (0.7%qtr, +0.12ppt) and while not significant to the
overall result it is interesting to track the ongoing rise in motor vehicle prices (0.3%qtr) despite a very
competitive market with Chinese manufacturers rapidly gaining market share over legacy brands.

It is worth noting that food was stronger than we expected (0.7% vs 0.5%) with a robust 1.7% increase
in restaurant meals and a solid 1.2% increase in take away & fast foods outweighing the –0.4% decline
in fresh fruit & vegetables and meat & seafoods. Overall, market services ex volatile prices increased a
very solid 1.2%qtr but with the annual pace easing to 3.2%yr from 3.36%yr.
Outside of housing the other main downside surprise in the quarter was the –0.6% fall in clothing &
footwear (+0.3% expected) with prices falling across just about all categories of garments, footwear
and accessories.
September Quarter Trimmed Mean

### Page 9

Recall that the TM is calculated from seasonally adjust consumption expenditure classes so any
numbers quoted here may not be the same as the original estimates quoted from the CPI. The TM gain
of 1.0% in the quarter was slightly stronger than we had expected even though dwellings (which have a
weight of almost 11% in the TM) was a touch softer at 1.0% compared to the 1.1% we had pencilled in.
Rents (1.0%, 10% weight) also made a solid contribution as did restaurant meals (1.7%qr, 6% weight),

take away & fast food (1.2%, 4% weight), other financial service (1.0%, 6% weight). Domestic travel
was in the TM (1.0%, 4% weight) as was medical & hospital service (1.0%, weight 5%). The range of
the TM was from 0.0% (motor vehicles) to 1.7% (beer).
What does this mean for our December quarter forecast and into 2027?
We are working though the monthly results to see what changes they may imply for individual
component inflationary momentum. Our preliminary analysis suggests that the softer trend in dwellings

is not enough, on its own, to change our Q4 CPI forecast of 1.3%. It does, however, suggest that if all
else held equal (which is not the case as there is likely to be some offsetting stronger gains elsewhere)
the moderation in dwelling inflation presents a very modest downside risk our Q4 TM estimate of 0.9%.
We are still working the numbers and will publish an updated inflation profile as soon as we can. In
addition, the overall momentum in underlying inflationary pressures suggest the downside risk to the

TM from dwellings is more likely to appear in 2027 than in the last quarter of 2026.

The stock market reacted to the Democrats’ midterm election sweep in
zig-zag fashion, with a crisis-aversion relief rally on Wednesday, a plunge
on Thursday as the thought of AI data center roadblocks chilled investor
optimism, and a bit of a recovery Friday as leveler heads prevailed (speed
bumps yes, roadblocks no). … Also: The AI race faces three intensifying
constraints—financial, political, and physical, the first two becoming acute.
Will the boom continue? Elias and Ed share why they think it will but at a
slower pace. … And: AI is making, not taking, jobs—at least so far. They

also share four cheery takeaways from the employment report for

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
It has been an action-packed few weeks for markets. The US
2026 mid-term elections saw a repudiation of President Trump
with the Democrats now set to take control over both houses
of Congress. The outcome and easing Middle East tensions

saw Brent crude oil fall from just under US$120/bbl to roughly
US$95/bbl, back around the post-Iran-conflict average. The
associated easing in inflation fears has supported a continued
descent in bond yields and rally in risk sentiment – albeit with
significant ‘wrinkles’ around a high US inflation read and an
AI-led pull-back in equity markets that has been compounded
by the sudden resignation of NVIDIA’s CEO.
Many central banks have already begun re-tightening policy

– with the Bank of Canada and Bank of England the latest
additions to the pack – but what comes next will differ widely
across jurisdictions. In the US, the combination of strong
activity and sticky inflation have led FOMC members to more
strongly advocate for further tightening. We have added a
January hike to the December move already in our forecasts,
before a long pause and eventual cuts in 2028.
Europe shares has similar inflation concerns but not so much

strength on the growth front. The ECB sees activity near
trend and is uncomfortable with inflation at 3.8%yr, so we
expect a December hike as insurance. Unlike market pricing
though, we think this will be the last, with energy and
investment-related price pressures easing through 2027 and
scope to ease back towards neutral in 2028. China faces a
very different challenge – not too much demand but too little.
Industrial strength and technology investment are providing

support but we expect policy direction and stimulus will be
needed to turn household demand, with this expected to
come in 2027 and property the most likely catalyst.
The RBA meanwhile followed its September hike with another
in November, but the Governor’s call to ‘take stock’ signals a
December pause and we think the hiking phase is now more
clearly over. We continue to pencil in a move to cuts in the
second half of 2027. While a February hike is not completely

out of the question, policy is clearly restrictive and downside
risks are crystallising. Reflecting this, we have downgraded
our GDP, labour market and housing market forecasts for 2027.
Australia: The housing downturn has been sharper than
expected and is set to spill over more materially to the broader
economy. The scale of these spillovers should become clearer
over coming months, keeping the RBA on hold as downside
risks to growth build. Business investment remains a source

of resilience, led by data centres and renewables, but rising
costs and growing community opposition have seen some
projects shelved. Still, the supply-side picture is constructive,
with strong investment in the market sector driving capital
deepening and productivity gains, albeit uneven ones.
Commodities: The commodities complex was a story of two
halves in October, with sharp gains across energy offsetting
weakness elsewhere and lifting our broadest commodities

index 0.6%mth. Reflecting continued energy-market tightness,
we expect Brent to average US$101/bbl in Q4 and have raised
our Q4 Japanese LNG forecast to US$27.
Global FX Markets: Of late, risks have favoured the US
dollar and FOMC member concerns have been decidedly one-
sided towards higher inflation. The DXY has consequently
continued to test the upper limit of its 18-month range, but
remains inside it.
New Zealand: A busy month has included a tight – and

currently inconclusive – general election, a surprise hold by the
RBNZ in October and a somewhat disappointing Q3 labour
market report. However, the medium-term outlook remains little
changed, with a recovering economy and above-target inflation
likely to lead to further monetary tightening in coming months.
Pacific: Distance from major markets, small domestic
economies and a heavy reliance on imported fuel and food
mean global disruptions tend to reach the Pacific at a higher

cost. However, most Pacific countries face the latest shock
with somewhat better buffers.
United States: Data suggests the US carried strong
momentum in to H2 2026. But questions are being asked over
the profitability of AI deployment, household real incomes are
trending flat to down, and term interest rates continue to weigh.
China: China GDP surprised to the upside in Q3, keeping
authorities on track for 2026, but more importantly offering a
little more time to accelerate domestic demand in to 2027.

Asia: We expect the BoJ to raise its policy rate by 25bps to
1.50% in December, before gradually taking it to 2.0% through
2027. The case for further tightening rests on the evidence that
firms are more willing to raise prices, labour scarcity supports
persistent wage growth, which should in turn support spending.
Europe & UK: The ECB left its key policy rates unchanged in
October. Another hike is forecast in December. Against market
pricing, we expect this will prove the last for the current cycle.

Taking stock
